| D-158 | **DEVIATION: the store's schema history is guarded through its committed schema files and a counted migration suite.** Upstream (SwiftData) keeps frozen copies of every shipped schema (`A/Store/FrozenSchemas.swift`, `A/App.swift:110-411`), opens stores written by its builds 33, 34, 43 and 45 (`AT/ShippedStoreMigrationTests.swift`, 11 tests; `AT/SchemaMigrationTests.swift`, 4 tests), and its release runbook (`docs/RUNBOOK_SCHEMA_MIGRATION_REHEARSAL.md`) has the person check the migration suite's executed-test count by hand. Version 1 may be re-pinned until the first GitHub Release. **Not ported, named:** the 11 tests of `AT/ShippedStoreMigrationTests.swift` and the 4 of `AT/SchemaMigrationTests.swift`. | Android has never shipped a store, so there is no older store to open; Room's exported JSON is the frozen copy of each version, and the build rewrites the current version's file on every compile, so a pinned hash is what catches an entity edited without a version bump. A test that silently stops running (JUnit 5 does not run a test function that returns a value; Gradle reports a skipped test as a success) is caught by the count, not by a person reading it. | E6a (done): `jvmTest/StoreMigrationTest.kt`, `jvmTest/SchemaHistoryGuardTest.kt`, `store/schemas/RELEASED.sha256`, `scripts/store-gate-a.sh`; every later schema version adds its `N.json`, its migration and its `versionN…` test, and pins the released file. |

| D-160 | **DEVIATION: the sleep store is its own class, and hands back values.** `SleepStore` sits beside `LocalStore` and `BlobStore` over the same database (upstream: one `LocalStore`). Every call takes `now` and `zone`, one zone for every day boundary (upstream: the wall clock and `Calendar.current`), and every instant is cut to the stored millisecond before it is compared. Stage minutes past `Int.MAX_VALUE` fail the save (`Math.toIntExact`; upstream's `Int` is 64-bit, the columns 32-bit). Here `01 00 00` is written only after the CCCD write's own callback reports success and after the Device Information reads, so the MAC is known first. A rejected descriptor write closes the connection and no command is written. | The ring answers `01 00 00` with its `81 00` challenge on the notify characteristic: sent before notifications are on, the challenge is lost and the link never authenticates. On Android the descriptor write is an operation with its own callback, so waiting for it costs one round trip. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/BringUpTest.kt` (`authStartsOnlyOnceTheNotificationDescriptorWriteIsConfirmed`, `aRejectedNotificationDescriptorWriteNeverStartsAuth`; both red when `01 00 00` is written after the descriptor write is submitted rather than answered, or when a rejected answer counts as confirmed). |
| D-184 | **DEVIATION (improvement): one GATT operation at a time, each finished by its own callback.** Upstream writes whenever a caller asks (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:4733-4768`, no queue) and reads the Device Information characteristics as they are discovered (`:4804-4811`), leaving the ordering to CoreBluetooth. Android allows one outstanding GATT operation per connection and refuses another. Here every operation (connect, discovery, MTU exchange, descriptor write, read, write) goes through one queue: the next starts only when the previous one's callback for the same connection has arrived; a refused submit or an error status closes the connection. Every write is a write with response, and the queue moves on at its callback. | A second operation submitted early is refused by Android and its command is lost without a trace. | E7 (done): the queue — `ble/jvmTest/BringUpTest.kt`, with `ble/jvmTestFixtures/FakeGatt.kt` failing any test that submits a second operation early (`ble/jvmTest/FakeGattTest.kt`); the suite goes red when the queue's one-in-flight check is removed. A timeout for every operation (D-185); acknowledgements ahead of queued feature writes (D-190). |
| D-185 | **DEVIATION (improvement): every GATT operation has a timeout, and any failure closes the connection.** Upstream has no operation timeout: it writes and returns (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:4733-4768`), and CoreBluetooth's connect (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:519-563`) never expires. Here each operation waits at most its own time: a direct connect 35 s (over Android's own 30 s), a standing connection without limit, discovery 10 s, the MTU exchange 5 s, the descriptor write 5 s, a read 5 s, a write 5 s; making a bond waits 40 s (30 s of pairing plus the user's answer; D-193). A timeout, an error status, a refused call or a drop fails the operation — a feature write answers `SendResult.Failed(TIMED_OUT)`, `Failed(GATT_ERROR)` or `Failed(LINK_LOST)`, and the writes waiting behind it `Failed(LINK_LOST)` — closes the connection and schedules the next attempt (D-186). A timer of a closed connection never fires on the next one. | Operations run one at a time (D-184), so one callback that never comes would stop the queue for good; and an ATT request unanswered for 30 s leaves the connection unusable anyway. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/OpTimeoutTest.kt` (each operation still waiting 1 ms before its timeout and failed at it), `ble/jvmTest/LinkTimeoutsGuardTest.kt` (every value pinned to a literal typed in the test), `ble/jvmTest/DisconnectMidOpTest.kt`; red when any timeout moves by 1 s, when a standing connection gets a timeout, or when the write in flight is left waiting at a drop. |
| D-186 | **DEVIATION (improvement): reconnect is direct first, then a standing connection.** Upstream re-issues one CoreBluetooth connect after each backoff delay (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:1035-1099`), and that connect never expires. Android has two kinds: a direct connect, fast but scanning hard and giving up after 30 s, and a standing `autoConnect` connection, cheap but slow and without limit. Here the attempts go to the same address at upstream's delays as direct connects; once three reconnects have failed, or a connect fails with status 147 or with 133 at least 20 s after it started (the ring is out of reach), the next attempt is one standing connection with no timeout (`LinkState.WaitingForRing`); a standing connection that fails before connecting goes back to direct without adding to the count. A connection that never connected publishes no teardown. | Direct connects repeated all night against a sleeping ring would scan hard for hours; a standing connection alone would make the first connection and short drops slow. Android's own guidance is direct first, then `autoConnect`. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/ReconnectPolicyTest.kt` (a 133 at 19.999 s retries direct, at 20 s arms the standing connection; 147 at once; the standing connection only after three failed reconnects; a failed standing connection keeps the count), `ble/jvmTest/OpTimeoutTest.kt` (1 s, 5 s, 30 s to the same address, nothing but connect, discovery and close called), `ble/jvmTest/StabilityResetTest.kt`. The background delay cap and background reconnects: E11. |
| D-187 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a GATT call that throws fails its operation instead of stopping the link.** Kotlin-only: upstream's CoreBluetooth calls do not throw. The Android adapter catches `SecurityException` and `IllegalArgumentException`; any other exception from a call into the GATT port (connect, discovery, MTU, descriptor, read, write, close) counts as a refused call: the operation fails, the connection is closed and the link reconnects. The bond state read has no "refused" answer, so the adapter does not catch there: no device, a revoked permission (`SecurityException`) or a code Android does not define throws, and the link counts it as a failed read, not as "not bonded": at the bond step it closes and reconnects without `createBond`; at the bond timeout the pairing ends `PairingFailed(BOND_TIMED_OUT)` as it would without the read; for the early-drop count it is not a bonded ring, so the count starts again. A bond broadcast with a missing or undefined state is ignored. An `Error` is not caught, so a test double's assertion still fails its test. | An uncaught exception would end the link's event loop; in the app's supervisor scope it would reach the thread's handler and crash the process. An unreadable bond state read as "not bonded" would ask Android to bond and show `PairingFailed(BOND_REQUEST_REJECTED)`, which the user cannot fix by pairing. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/PortFailureTest.kt` (4 tests, all red when the exception escapes); `ble/jvmTest/BondFailureTest.kt` (a throwing bond state closes and reconnects without `createBond`); `ble/jvmTest/BondStateCodeTest.kt` (5: Android's three codes, null and an undefined code throw, a broadcast's missing or undefined code is ignored, the codes pinned to literals). |
| D-188 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a challenge that arrives before the MAC is settled waits for it.** Upstream answers every `81 00 <challenge>` at once, with the legacy fixed reply when it has no MAC yet (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:5292-5294`), and when the System ID read lands afterwards it writes `01 00 00` again so the ring challenges anew (`:4839-4849`). Here the MAC is settled once the System ID (`0x2a23`) has been read, or at discovery when the ring has none; a challenge that arrives earlier is kept, in arrival order, and answered on the acknowledgement lane (D-190) as soon as the MAC is settled. It waits even when the device address already parses, because the System ID wins when the two disagree: `LinkInfo.mac` is then the System ID's MAC and `LinkInfo.macMismatch` is true. Challenges held on a connection that drops are never answered on the next one. Every challenge is answered, a repeated one too, and its trailer is not checked, as upstream. | A reply computed from the wrong MAC is dropped by the ring without a word, so answering early with a guess or the device address could leave the link unauthenticated; asking again costs a round trip and relies on the ring re-challenging. With the Device Information reads before `01 00 00` (D-183) an early challenge is rare, so this is a safety net. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/AuthRobustnessTest.kt` (11 tests; red when an early challenge is dropped, when it is answered from the address before the System ID is read, when the mismatch is not reported, when the address wins, and when held challenges survive a reconnect). |
| D-189 | **DEVIATION (improvement): every frame waits in one buffer per connection for one collector at a time, and a teardown counts what it drops.** Upstream handles each frame where it arrives (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:4871-5298`) and keeps what it acknowledged (`:5141-5187`). Here every frame but the `81 00` challenge goes, in arrival order, into an unbounded buffer behind `RingLink.frames`; it waits there while nobody collects. One collector at a time: a collection started while another runs throws `IllegalStateException`, and a collector that stops or is cancelled leaves every frame it did not take for the next one. `teardowns` works the same way. When a connection is torn down its frames still waiting are dropped and their number is reported as `LinkTeardown.undeliveredFrames`. Measured with kotlinx-coroutines 1.11.0, the library's own channel flows do not keep these promises: `receiveAsFlow` lets a second collector in and splits the frames between the two; `consumeAsFlow` cancels the channel when its collector stops, after which every frame is refused without a word; and a channel receiver cancelled just after a frame was handed to it loses that frame. | The link has already acknowledged most frames to the ring, which advances past them for good, so a frame lost on the phone is lost for good. A 2 000-page burst with no collector is held whole (2 020 frames with its 20 heartbeats, each acknowledged once). | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/FrameBufferTest.kt` (12 tests: no collector, a collector that stops, one cancelled mid-burst and one cancelled between a frame's arrival and its own wake-up, a second collector, a slow collector cut off by a teardown, the next connection's empty buffer, the 2 000-page burst with no collector and with a slow one), `ble/jvmTest/FrameDeliveryHazardTest.kt` (the library measurements), `ble/jvmTest/FakeRingLinkTest.kt` (the test fixture keeps the same behaviour); red when frames are dropped while nobody collects, when the buffer is a channel, when the one-collector check is removed, when the count is 0 or the buffer is not cleared at teardown. |
| D-190 | **DEVIATION (improvement): page and heartbeat acknowledgements go ahead of every waiting feature write.** Upstream writes each acknowledgement (`c7 00 00` for `0x47`, `cc 00 00` for `0x4C`, `cd 00 00` for `0x4D`, `91 00 00` for `0x11`) the moment the frame arrives (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:5137`, `:5185`, `:5192`, `:5249`), with no queue. Android runs one GATT operation at a time (D-184), so here the acknowledgements and the auth reply go on a lane of their own, taken before the bring-up steps and feature writes at the next free slot; the operation in flight is never cut into. Each acknowledgement is written exactly once per frame, in arrival order, decided by the opcode alone, so a page that fails its checksum or is cut short is still acknowledged; frames of any other opcode are not. The bytes come from `Command.pageAck47`, `pageAck4C`, `pageAck4D` and `heartbeatAck`. | The ring sends its next page only after the acknowledgement of the last, so an acknowledgement stuck behind queued feature writes stalls the transfer. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/AckLaneTest.kt` (6 tests: real `0x4c` pages, a truncated `0x47` page, a short `0x4d` frame and `0x11` heartbeats, interleaved, with feature writes queued before the burst), `ble/jvmTest/FrameBufferTest.kt` (2 000 pages, each acknowledged once); red when the feature lane is taken first, when only frames with a valid checksum are acknowledged, and when other opcodes are acknowledged too. |
| D-191 | **DEVIATION (improvement): `send` refuses what the ring must not get, as a value.** Upstream writes whatever a caller asks (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:4733-4768`). Here `RingLink.send` answers `SendResult.Refused(reason)`, writes nothing and never throws, checking in this order: before `LinkState.Authenticated`, everything (`NOT_AUTHENTICATED`); `01 00 00` and any `01 01 …`, which belong to the link's own auth exchange (`AUTH_COMMAND_RESERVED`); a command outside the `0x01` family while the phone holds no bond with the ring (`NOT_BONDED`); a history sync open, `0x02 …`, while the ATT MTU is below 246 (`HISTORY_UNSAFE`). `LinkInfo.bonded` is true once the bring-up's bond step found or made the bond (D-193) and then follows every bond change on the live connection (a bond removed in Settings, or being made again, refuses data at once); `LinkInfo.historySafe` is an ATT MTU of at least 246. | The ring ignores data from a phone it is not bonded to (`docs/PROTOCOL.md` §0), and a second `01 00 00` or a stray `01 01` would restart or break the auth exchange. A 243-byte history frame (`0x50`, `docs/PROTOCOL.md` §5.5.1) needs an ATT MTU of 246; on a smaller one the frame would arrive cut short, after the link had acknowledged it. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/SendRefusalTest.kt` (10 tests, one per rule, the order between the bond and MTU rules, MTU 245 and 246, a bond still being made, an accepted command answered only at its write callback, and no command in any state making `send` throw), `ble/jvmTest/NoDataBeforeBondTest.kt` (4: nothing written while the bond is made, every data command refused and none written once the bond is lost on a live connection, data again once it is back), `ble/jvmTest/MtuGateTest.kt` (6: the MTU rule on a ring bonded during the bring-up, 23, 245, 246 and 517, an MTU error, the next connection); red when any rule or its order is removed or moved, when the MTU threshold moves by one, and when bond changes on a live connection are ignored. |
| D-192 | **DEVIATION: the link never answers a challenge without the ring's MAC.** Upstream falls back to the legacy fixed reply `Command.status1` when it has no MAC (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:5294`); that command is not ported (D-1). Here, when neither the System ID nor the device address gives a MAC (the address is not six pairs of ASCII hex digits, D-11), no auth reply is written at all and the link stays `Authenticating` until, 10 s after notifications were confirmed, it shows `NotStreaming` (D-194). | The fixed reply is right only for one captured ring and challenge; for any other it is a wrong reply the ring drops without a word, which looks like a working link that never streams. Android always reports a well-formed device address, so this case means a corrupt remembered ring. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/AuthRobustnessTest.kt` (`withNoMacFromEitherSourceNoChallengeIsEverAnsweredWithAGuess`, `anAddressThatIsNotSixPairsOfAsciiHexDigitsIsNeverParsed`: fullwidth and Arabic-Indic digits, wrong group counts, other separators), `ble/jvmTest/NotStreamingTest.kt` (`aLinkWhoseMacNeverSettlesShowsNotStreamingAfter10Seconds`). |
| D-193 | **DEVIATION (addition): the link makes the bond after discovery, waits for it, and never sends data to an unbonded ring.** CoreBluetooth has no bonding call: iOS bonds on its own when the peripheral asks, so upstream has no such step. Android bonds only when asked or when the ring sends a Security Request, and the ring ignores data from a phone it is not bonded to (`docs/PROTOCOL.md` §0). Here the bring-up has a bond step between discovery and the ATT MTU exchange, on the connection already open: bonded goes on; already bonding (the ring asked, or a bond left over) waits and never calls `createBond`, which would collide with the bond in progress; not bonded calls `createBond` once and waits. While it waits the link shows `PairingNeeded` (Android shows the user a pairing prompt). The wait ends on bonded (the bring-up goes on with exactly the steps and bytes of a ring bonded from the start), on bonding falling back to not bonded (`PairingFailed(BOND_NOT_COMPLETED)`), on a refused or throwing `createBond` (`PairingFailed(BOND_REQUEST_REJECTED)`), after 40 s (`PairingFailed(BOND_TIMED_OUT)`, unless the bond state read once more at 40 s is bonded: then the broadcast was lost and the bring-up goes on), or on a drop (the usual reconnect). A pairing failure closes the connection and is not retried; the link waits in `PairingFailed` until `connect()`. A "not bonded" before the bond started is a stale broadcast and is ignored; a bond state that cannot be read closes the connection and reconnects (D-187) without asking. Bond changes reach the link as events on its one event channel and keep `LinkInfo.bonded` current on a live connection. The link never removes a bond and has no call for it. | A data command to an unbonded ring is dropped without a word and looks delivered. Retrying a declined pairing would put the prompt in front of the user again and again. A second `createBond` during a bond the ring started can make the pairing fail. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/BondFlowTest.kt` (6), `ble/jvmTest/BondFailureTest.kt` (12: each way the wait ends, still waiting at 39 999 ms and failed at 40 000 ms, no retry for ten minutes, `connect()` asking again, a late bond after the failure, a bond whose broadcast never arrived found at 40 000 ms), `ble/jvmTest/NoDataBeforeBondTest.kt` (4); red when the bond step is skipped, when `createBond` is called on a bonded or bonding ring, when a pairing failure reconnects, when the timeout moves by 1 s, and when a stale "not bonded" fails the pairing. The Android bond receiver (`ble/androidMain/AndroidLinkBroadcasts.kt`) reports bond changes for the link's ring only. |
| D-194 | **DEVIATION: not streaming is one of the link's states, not a flag beside the others.** Upstream arms its watchdog when the subscription is confirmed (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:5389-5391`), flips the `notStreaming` flag if no frame other than `0x81` came within 10 s (`firstFrameTimeout`, `:264`; `:1190-1205`), clears it on the first such frame (`:4884-4888`), and sets it at once when the subscription fails (`:5382-5386`). Here the same 10 s are a timer on the link's event loop started by the confirmed notification descriptor write, with the same rule for what counts as data; running out shows `LinkState.NotStreaming`, one state among the others rather than a flag beside `ready`. A link that receives nothing at all (its MAC never settled, D-192) shows it too. The connection stays open; the first data frame afterwards moves the link on to `Authenticated` (or back to `Preparing` if `01 00 00` was not written yet), and writing `01 00 00` does not hide `NotStreaming`. The 10 s belong to their connection: a drop cancels them. A failed subscription closes the connection and reconnects instead (D-183). | The symptom of a ring that did not accept this phone is a link that is up and silent; the app needs to tell it from a link still being prepared, and upstream's separate flags could hold together in combinations that mean nothing. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/NotStreamingTest.kt` (10: still `Authenticating` at 9 999 ms after the confirmation and `NotStreaming` at 10 000 ms with only auth frames; a data frame at 9 990 ms keeps it away; recovery; no MAC; a streaming ring never; the 10 s of a dropped connection never reaching the next one; slow Device Information reads); red when the 10 s count from connect, when a data frame does not stop them, when an auth frame counts as data, and when the wait moves by 1 s. |
| D-195 | **DEVIATION (addition): three early drops in a row of a bonded ring show a suspected lost bond.** CoreBluetooth reports no such case, so upstream has none. On Android 16 a ring that has lost the bond (factory reset, paired elsewhere) still reads as bonded, and the system disconnects every connection at once after showing its own dialog. Here a connection Android reports disconnected, to a ring it still reports bonded, that had connected and dropped before discovery was done or less than 2 s after connecting, counts as early. Three in a row show `LinkState.BondLostSuspected` instead of `Reconnecting`; two do not. The count lives on the link across its connections: a connection through discovery and up 2 s resets it, and so does an early drop of a ring that is not bonded. The link's own timeouts and error statuses never count. Reconnecting goes on after the usual delay, and the state shows again while the drops go on. The bond is never removed by the app. | Recovery is the user's: forget the ring in system Settings and pair again. The app can only point there, and must not do so for a ring that is merely slow, which would have the user remove a working bond. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/BondLostSuspectedTest.kt` (11, every case through one link: two not enough, three before discovery, three within 2 s of connecting, 1 999 ms counts and 2 000 ms not, 2 100 ms after discovery resets the count, a discovery finishing after 2 s still resets it, drops still in discovery after 2 s count, an unbonded ring never, timeouts and errors never, reconnecting goes on, a healthy connection afterwards); red when three becomes two, when the window moves either way, when a reset is removed, when an unbonded ring counts, when every failure counts, and when reconnecting stops. |
| D-196 | **DEVIATION (addition): the link keeps a step-by-step record of its connections, with nothing that identifies the ring.** Upstream logs to the system log (`ringLog`, e.g. `A/BLE/RingSession.swift:1201`), which a tester reads from a Mac with the phone on a cable. The Android phone is never on a cable, so the link offers `LinkDiagnostics.diagnostics` beside `RingLink` (reached with a cast; the public interface is unchanged): the last 64 `LinkDiagnostic(sinceConnectMillis, event, detail)` entries of the link, oldest first. Each bring-up step started and finished, where the auth MAC came from, each bond change marked before or after the link called `createBond`, failures, timeouts, disconnects, `NotStreaming`, a suspected lost bond, each close with its reason and undelivered frame count, each scheduled reconnect. Times count from the connection attempt's start on the link's clock. Acknowledgements and feature writes are recorded only when they fail. No entry holds the address, the MAC, the System ID or frame bytes. | A tester needs to see how pairing and the bring-up went on the phone itself (in particular whether the ring asked to pair before the app did), and a copy of the record must be safe to share. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/LinkDiagnosticsTest.kt` (9: order, virtual-time stamps per attempt, details, before and after `createBond`, the 64 kept, the factory's link offering them, and a privacy check over every kind of entry with an address and System ID that disagree), `ble/jvmTest/FakeRingLinkTest.kt` (the fixture keeps the same bound); red when 65 are kept, when the address, the MAC or frame bytes reach an entry, when the clock is not reset at a reconnect, and when the factory hides the record. |
| D-197 | **DEVIATION (addition): a ring also matches a scan by advertising its data service.** Upstream's foreground scan matches the advertised name only (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:913-914`, `Transport.matchesRingName`); its service filter is used only for background scans (`:301-306`). Here an advertisement is a ring when its name starts with a ring prefix OR its service UUIDs include the ring's data service `8327ad99-…` (compared in lower case with `Locale.ROOT`), so a ring advertising the service without a name is still found. The scan stays unfiltered: Android has no `ScanFilter` for a name prefix. | Whether the ring advertises the service is not known; matching either way costs nothing and finds a ring whose advertisement carries no name. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/RingScannerTest.kt` (`aRingThatAdvertisesOnlyTheDataServiceIsFound`, `anAdvertisementThatMatchesNeitherWayIsIgnored`: another service, the notify characteristic's UUID, a lower-case or embedded prefix, no name); red when the service clause is dropped or compared case-sensitively. |
| D-198 | **DEVIATION: a ring is remembered by its address and address type, and only a well-formed address is offered.** Upstream remembers CoreBluetooth's peripheral identifier (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:89-175`), a per-phone UUID that hides the address. Android reconnects by address, so `RememberedRing` holds the address in upper case and its type: the one Android reports in the scan result (Android 15 and later; `ADDRESS_TYPE_PUBLIC` / `ADDRESS_TYPE_RANDOM`, anything else not counted), else `RANDOM` when the top two bits of the first byte are set (a random static address) and `PUBLIC` otherwise, Android's own default for a device built from an address. The link rebuilds the device with `getRemoteLeDevice(address, type)` on every connection; once bonded, Android's bond record supplies the true type. An advertisement whose address is not six pairs of ASCII hex digits is never offered. | A random address rebuilt as public can fail to connect before the ring is bonded. A malformed address could be neither rebuilt nor turned into the MAC that auth needs (D-11). | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/RingScannerTest.kt` (`theAddressTypeAndroidReportsIsKept`, `withoutAReportedTypeTheTopTwoBitsOfTheFirstByteDecide`, `addressesAreKeptUpperCaseAndTheSameRingInEitherCaseIsOneRing`, `anAddressThatIsNotSixPairsOfAsciiHexDigitsIsNeverOffered`: fullwidth and Arabic-Indic digits, other separators, five groups, empty); red when the reported type is ignored, when the guess reads one bit, and when a malformed address is accepted. Synthetic addresses only. |
| D-199 | **DEVIATION (addition): the scanner keeps the last matched advertisement, with nothing that identifies the ring.** Upstream logs to the system log, read from a Mac over a cable. The Android phone is never on a cable, so the scanner offers `ScanDiagnostics.lastMatch` beside `RingScanner` (reached with a cast; the public interface is unchanged): the latest matched advertisement of any scan of the scanner as `ScanDiagnostic(addressType, rawScanRecord, rssi)`, kept after its scan ends. The bytes are copied in and on every read, equality is by content, and its text form shows the record's length, not its bytes. It holds no address. | A tester needs to see what the ring advertises and its address type on the phone itself; a copy of it must not carry the address. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/ScanDiagnosticsTest.kt` (8: offered through a cast, recorded per match with the reported or guessed type, untouched by a non-match, kept across scans, copies in and out at both the scanner and the value, content equality, no address in any field or the text form); red when a copy is removed, when equality is by reference, and when the text form shows the bytes. |
| D-200 | **DEVIATION: a scan Android refuses to start ends at once with `Failed(-1)`.** Upstream keeps a scan asked for before Bluetooth is on and starts it once Bluetooth is on (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:313-321`, `pendingScan`). Here a collection whose scan Android refuses to start (Bluetooth off, no scanner, the permission missing or revoked) gets `ScanUpdate.Failed(-1)` and ends; `-1` is no `ScanCallback` error code. | The app checks `BTAvailability` before it scans and owns the retry; a scan that silently waits would hold the pairing screen without telling the user why. | E7 (done): `ble/jvmTest/RingScannerTest.kt` (`aScanAndroidRefusesToStartFailsAtOnceWithMinusOne`). |

| D-230 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a frame no part of the app handles is counted and logged, never dropped silently.** Upstream's frame switch ends in `default: break` (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:5334`), and a frame that then fails `Frame.parse` returns without a trace (`:5336`). Here every frame goes to the handler registered for its opcode. An opcode with no handler (history pages `0x4c` / `0x47` / `0x4d` while the app does not sync yet, any unknown opcode) is counted per opcode and logged with the opcode only, never the frame's bytes; an empty frame is counted too. The only opcode the app ignores on purpose is `0x11`, which `:ble` already answers with `91 00 00`. | A stray history page that vanishes without a trace is data loss nobody notices; a count makes it visible. | E8 (done): `app/test/FrameDispatcherTest.kt` (exact counts per opcode, one log line each without the frame's bytes), `app/test/RingSessionTracerTest.kt` (the same through the session controller); red when the count is dropped. |
| D-231 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a frame handler that throws is contained, and the next frame is still routed.** Kotlin-only: upstream's frame handling does not throw. The link's frames take one collector at a time, and the app's session holds one collection for the whole session and never starts another; an exception escaping the collector ends that collection, and the frames behind it then wait in the link for a collector that never comes (`app/test/CollectorThrowHazardTest.kt`: the frame queued behind the failing one is not delivered, and only a fresh collection would receive it). The dispatcher calls each handler inside its own try/catch: a failure is counted per opcode and logged with the opcode and the exception's class only; `CancellationException` is rethrown. | One bad frame must not silence the ring for the rest of the session, and the count keeps the failure visible. | E8 (done): `app/test/FrameDispatcherTest.kt` (a throwing handler, then the next frame routed; counts per opcode; cancellation rethrown), `app/test/SingleCollectorTest.kt` (the same inside the controller's collection); red when the catch is narrowed. |
| D-232 | **DEVIATION: no `01 00 00` from the app — not at the start of a live measure, nor as a keepalive or status-refresh prime.** Upstream writes `01 00 00` before the live entry (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:1074`), to prime the keepalive (`:1234`) and as a status-refresh fallback (`:4699`), each time to draw the ring's `81 00` challenge so the auth reply runs again. Here the link runs auth on every connection itself and refuses `01 00 00` and `01 01 …` from callers (`AUTH_COMMAND_RESERVED`, D-191); the live entry starts at `d0 00 00`. | A second `01 00 00` restarts the auth exchange the link owns; the refusal would end every measure before it began. Whether the ring enters live mode without the prime is checked on a real ring before release. | E8 (done): `app/test/WholeSessionForbiddenWritesTest.kt` (a whole session — connect, idle keepalive ticks, measure, re-tap, switch, stop, the status refresh, reconnect, measure, teardown — writes no `01 00 00`, no `01 01 …` and no `02 …`); red when `01 00 00` is written before the entry. `app/test/KeepaliveTickerTest.kt`: the keepalive and the refresh write `d0 00 00` and nothing else. |
| D-233 | **DEVIATION: `07 00 00` only as the third write of a live entry — no idle `07` keepalive and no `07` in the status refresh.** Upstream's idle keepalive writes `07 00 00` outside its sleep window (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:1251-1255`) and its status-refresh ladder writes `07` (`:4677-4722`). Here `07 00 00` follows a mode write 250 ms earlier and a `d0 00 00` 500 ms earlier, every time. | Upstream itself disagrees on whether `07` walks the history pointer (`:4680-4683` against `docs/PROTOCOL.md` §3); the app has no history drain to bank pages it might start, so it never sends `07` where it is not needed. `d0 00 00` alone brings the `0x10` descriptor. | E8 (done): `app/test/WholeSessionForbiddenWritesTest.kt` (each of the session's five `07 00 00` writes has its mode 250 ms and its `d0` 500 ms before it; every other `d0 00 00` is a keepalive or refresh, none while a measure runs); red when the poll loop writes `07 00 00`. `app/test/KeepaliveTickerTest.kt`: red when the keepalive writes `07 00 00` instead of `d0 00 00` (10 tests, 7 failed across both classes). |
| D-234 | **DEFERRED: saving live readings.** Upstream saves the settled heart rate and the last SpO₂ when a measure stops (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:3430-3477`), at the pin through the forward-only sync cursor — the bug upstream a356344 fixed by saving live readings outside it. Here the reading stays on its card for the session only. | Nothing reads saved live readings before the history and Health Connect work, and saving them needs a `:store` change made with the cursor rule of a356344. | E9 (store) and E10 (Health Connect), with a356344's no-cursor rule and its tests. |
| D-235 | **DEVIATION (improvement): the Measure readout of upstream's later commits — a large newest reading, the low–high of the whole measurement, Stop on the live card.** At the pin the Measure buttons sit on the vitals table rows (`A/VitalsTableView.swift:319-376`) and the chart shows the value inside it. Upstream's later commits 5c40605 (large newest-value readout), 6e93d53 (low–high over the whole measurement, not the chart's 120 points) and e0b2294 (Stop reachable from the live card) are adopted: a Live card with the newest reading in large type, "lo–hi so far" once there are two readings, the chart, and Stop, above one card per metric. | The pin's readout showed whichever frame came last with no context; the later layout shows the spread and lets the user stop where they look. | E8 (done): `app/test/MeasurePresenterTest.kt` (readout, range, progress lines and card captions per state), `app/test/LiveSessionTest.kt` (the range keeps a low the chart dropped), `app/androidTest/LiveCardRenderTest.kt`. |
| D-236 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a measure ends exactly at its budget, without one more poll.** Upstream checks the budget only after writing each poll (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:1149-1180`): with the budget armed 750 ms after the entry, heart rate's 90 s lands on a poll and one useless `95 00 00` goes out before the stop; SpO₂'s 45 s falls between polls, so it runs 1 s over. Here the poll loop sleeps until the next poll or the budget, whichever is first, and stops at the budget without writing. | The budget is what the user was told; a poll whose answer nobody reads is a wasted write. | E8 (done): `app/test/LiveMeasureBudgetTest.kt` (still measuring 1 ms before 90 s and 45 s, stopped at them, no poll at or after the budget; a re-tap and a switch restart it); red when the heart-rate budget is 91 s. |
| D-237 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a tap while a live entry is being written does not interleave a second entry.** Upstream re-arms on a same-mode tap by writing `d0` → mode → `07` at once (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:3369-3390`), even while the first entry is still being written, so the two sequences interleave. Here a same-mode tap during the entry does nothing (the entry already under way is the fresh read), and a switch to the other mode cancels the entry and starts it again in the new mode. | Interleaved entries put a `07 00 00` after the wrong mode write. | E8 (done): `app/test/LiveMeasureBudgetTest.kt` (`aReTapWhileTheEntryIsStillBeingWrittenDoesNotWriteASecondEntry`), `app/test/LiveMeasureStopTest.kt` (`switchingModeWhileTheEntryIsBeingWrittenRestartsTheEntryInTheNewMode`); red when a tap during the entry re-arms. |
| D-238 | **DEVIATION (improvement): a refused or failed live-measure write, or a disconnect mid-measure, ends the measure with its reason on the card.** Upstream's `write(_:)` returns nothing (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:4733-4768`), so a write that fails leaves the poll loop running until its budget, and a disconnect stops the measure without a word (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:989-1012`). Here every write of the measure returns a `SendResult`; a refusal or failure at any entry step, re-entry or poll ends the measure at once and writes nothing more, and a torn-down connection or the link leaving `Authenticated` ends it with "the ring disconnected". Each reason has its own line on the card. | A measure that silently stops arriving looks like a ring that cannot read; the reason tells the user what to do. | E8 (done): `app/test/MeasureFailureSurfacedTest.kt` (each refusal and failure at each entry step, at a poll and at a re-entry), `app/test/LiveMeasureStopTest.kt` (teardown, `Reconnecting`, `NotStreaming`), `app/test/MeasurePresenterTest.kt` (the line for each); red when a refusal is treated as sent, and when the teardown or state stop is removed. |
| D-239 | **DEVIATION (improvement): every locked reading is a point on the live chart, a repeated value too.** Upstream feeds the chart from `.onChange(of: liveHR)` (`A/ContentView.swift:174-181`), which does not fire when a reading equals the one before, so a steady heart rate stops adding points and its low–high count. Here each locked frame is added with its arrival time. | A steady reading is a reading; the chart's time axis should show it. | E8 (done): `app/test/LiveSettledTest.kt` (`theChartAndRangeTakeEveryLockedFrameStampedWithItsArrival`: 82, 82, 61 at 3, 5 and 7 s are three points); red when a reading equal to the one before is skipped. |
| D-240 | **DEVIATION: the status refresh after a measure is one `d0 00 00` at once and one retry 30 s later, not upstream's `07` / `01` / `d0` ladder at the next idle tick.** Upstream queues a refresh when a live measure stops (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:3470`) and runs it at the next idle keepalive tick: `07 00 00`, 750 ms, then `01 00 00` + `07`, then `d0 00 00` + `07`, re-queued if no descriptor came (`:4677-4722`); during a live read that tick comes every 30 s (`KeepaliveCadence`'s active value). Here the refresh runs the moment the measure stops: one `d0 00 00`; if no descriptor with a battery arrives within 750 ms, one more `d0 00 00` 30 s later; then the 180 s cadence. The idle keepalive writes `d0 00 00` where upstream writes `07 00 00` by day (`:1251-1255`). | `d0 00 00` alone brings the `0x10` descriptor (`docs/PROTOCOL.md` §5.4); `01 00 00` is the link's (D-232) and `07` is kept to the live entry (D-233). One retry on the cadence upstream keeps around a live read bounds the extra writes to one. Whether the ring keeps the link and answers with `d0` alone is checked on a real ring before release. | E8 (done): `app/test/KeepaliveTickerTest.kt` (no retry when a descriptor arrives at 749 ms; one retry at 30 s when it arrives at 751 ms; the refresh after a budget stop; the cadence restarts from the refresh); red when the retry is dropped (9 tests, 1 failed). |
| D-241 | **DEVIATION: while connected, the rising-battery inference alone labels the battery "charging (inferred)" and turns the time line towards full; the bolt, the charger hint and Measure follow the charger byte only.** Upstream shows the charging bolt, the time to full and the charger hint from the decoded byte `[2] == 0x04` (`A/ContentView.swift:1135-1166`, `:1426-1440`) and uses the inference only to hide the time left (`:1171`) and for the reconnecting line (`:2241-2244`), so an inferred charge leaves its card with no time line at all. Here the bolt, the charger hint and the Measure block follow the byte alone, as upstream; when only the inference holds, the card adds the note "charging (inferred)" and the time line reads "estimating time to full…" (the charge history is folded with the byte, as upstream, so it stays estimating), instead of nothing. | A 1 % rise across two distinct readings is enough for the inference, so a worn ring can read as charging until the next drop; a guess must say it is one and must never block the app's core action. The first build let the inference also draw the hint and block Measure (a worn ring at 66 → 67 % lost Measure); this replaced it. The real-ring run checks how often the label appears on a worn ring. | E8 (done): `app/test/ChargingInferenceWiringTest.kt` (with the byte at `0x02`, 66 → 67 shows the label, no bolt, no hint, Measure enabled and starting; the byte at `0x04` with no rise blocks Measure; 66, 67, 66 drops the label; a repeated reading changes nothing), `app/androidTest/ConnectionCardRenderTest.kt` (the label drawn, Measure enabled); red when the inference blocks Measure, when the view model refuses Measure on it, or when it shows the charger hint (5 tests, 1 failed each). |
| D-242 | **DEVIATION: Measure is disabled while the charger byte says the ring is on its charger; upstream never blocks a manual measure.** Upstream's charger hint reads "auto-measure paused" and its comment says manual Measure is never blocked (`A/ContentView.swift:1426-1440`). Here, while connected, the descriptor's state byte is `0x04` and no measure runs, the card says "Ring is on the charger — Measure unavailable", both Measure buttons are disabled and the view model starts no measure; a running measure can still be stopped. The rising-battery inference never blocks Measure (D-241). | A ring on its charger is not on a finger: a measure there only ends in "Couldn't get a reading". A guess is not enough to take the button away. | E8 (done): `app/test/ConnectionCardStateTest.kt`, `app/test/ChargingInferenceWiringTest.kt`, `app/androidTest/ConnectionCardRenderTest.kt`; red when the buttons stay enabled (16 tests, 3 failed; on the emulator 6 tests, 1 failure), when the view model starts a measure while on the charger (16 tests, 2 failed), and when the inference blocks Measure (5 tests, 1 failed). |
| D-243 | **DEFERRED: keeping the battery histories and the charging inference across launches, and the "inferred charging" note on the reconnecting line.** Upstream saves the discharge and charge histories per ring (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:834-847`, `:5085-5101`) and the charging inference at teardown (`:213-221`) in `UserDefaults`, and appends " · inferred charging" to "Ring unreachable" from it (`A/ContentView.swift:2241-2254`). Here the readings live in memory for the session: a relaunch starts with "estimating time left…". | The app persists only its three settings until the store and the dashboard work; a saved history belongs with the battery's other stored data. | E9 (store) and E12 (dashboard). |
| D-244 | **DEVIATION (addition): a Disconnect button on the connected and not-streaming card.** Upstream's connected card has no button; Cancel / Stop reconnecting appear only while connecting (`A/ContentView.swift:1226-1238`). Here every link state has one action, and for a connected or not-streaming ring it is Disconnect (the remembered ring reconnects on the next launch). | Only one app can hold the ring; disconnecting lets the user hand it to another app without leaving this one, and a not-streaming ring often recovers on a fresh connection. | E8 (done): `app/test/LinkStatePresenterTest.kt` (`notStreamingSaysTheLinkIsOpenButNoDataCameYet`, `all13StatesHaveTheirOwnWordsAndAnAction`); 11 tests, 11 failed on a stub presenter. |
| D-245 | **DEVIATION: the battery reads as out of date at 120 s without a reading, not only after it.** Upstream's `batteryStale` is `now − batteryFetchedAt > 120` (`A/BLE/RingSession.swift:178-187`), checked whenever the view redraws. Here a timer marks the reading out of date at exactly 120 s and recounts its age each minute. | The edge is one millisecond; a timer needs one instant to fire at, and 120 s is the design's. | E8 (done): `app/test/BatteryStaleTest.kt` (`theBatteryIsFreshAt119999MillisecondsAndStaleAt120Seconds`); red when the threshold is 121 s (5 tests, 4 failed). |
| D-246 | **DEVIATION: the remembered ring is one value holding the address, its type and the advertised name, and a damaged value reads as no ring.** Upstream keeps a list of every ring's identifier and the active one in `UserDefaults`, with a one-time migration from an older single key (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:96-164`). Here `ring.remembered.v1` is one string, `ADDRESS\|TYPE\|NAME`: the address upper-case, the address type's name, and the name as `-` (none) or `b64:` and the Base64 of its UTF-8 bytes. A value that does not read back as all three exactly (a wrong type, a missing or extra field, an address that is not six pairs of ASCII hex digits, a name that is not Base64 of valid UTF-8) reads as no ring; an invalid address is refused on save. Addresses compare case-folded with `Locale.ROOT`. | Android reconnects by address and needs the address type; one ring at a time until the app supports several, and there is no older Android value to migrate. Written together, the three fields can never be half-saved, and a damaged file must never become a connection to an address nobody chose. | E8 (done): `app/test/RememberedRingStoreTest.kt`, `app/test/AppPrefsTest.kt`, `app/androidTest/SharedPreferencesKeyValuesTest.kt`; red when the digit check uses `Char.isDigit` (13 tests, 2 failed) and when a load trusts the stored address (13 tests, 6 failed). |
| D-247 | **DEVIATION: Nearby devices is asked for at the first Scan & connect, a saved flag tells "never asked" from "refused for good", and a refusal for good offers Settings and "Ask again".** Upstream creates its Bluetooth manager at the first tap, which shows iOS's prompt (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:313-314`), and for a denied or restricted permission shows "Allow Bluetooth in Settings" (`A/ContentView.swift:1278-1291`). Android reports "never asked" and "refused for good" alike, so `bt.permission.asked.v1` is saved when the dialog answers (and remembered for the session if the save fails). Never asked: the tap shows the dialog. Refused once: "Bluetooth not allowed" with "Allow Nearby devices". Refused for good (or a dismissed dialog): "Open settings" and "Ask again". A device policy that forbids Bluetooth: "Bluetooth is blocked by a device policy" with no button. One of the two permissions alone is not a grant. The permission is re-read on every resume; onboarding never asks. | Android's runtime-permission model: without the flag a fresh install would read as refused for good and offer Settings first; the dialog cannot be shown again after a second refusal; Android's guidance is to offer Settings only after it. | E8 (done): `app/test/PermissionMapperTest.kt`, `app/test/FirstTapSequenceTest.kt`, `app/androidTest/RingStatesRenderTest.kt`; red when "never asked" maps to denied (10 tests, 2 failed) and when the dialog's answer does not save the flag (12 tests, 4 failed). On an API 36.1 emulator: one refusal gave "Allow Nearby devices", a second (`USER_FIXED`) gave "Open settings" and "Ask again". |
| D-248 | **DEVIATION: Turn on Bluetooth shows Android's own dialog and the scan goes on once Bluetooth is on.** iOS lets no app turn Bluetooth on, so upstream's button shows an alert pointing to Control Center or Settings (`A/ContentView.swift:1249-1257`, `:1270-1277`). Here the button asks Android to turn it on (`BluetoothAdapter.ACTION_REQUEST_ENABLE`; apps targeting API 33 or later cannot call `enable()`); when Android says it is on and no link is active, the scan the user tapped for goes on. If no screen handles the request or Android refuses it, Bluetooth settings open instead. The card follows the adapter live through an `ACTION_STATE_CHANGED` receiver registered not exported. | Android offers to turn Bluetooth on in place, which saves the user a trip to Settings. | E8 (done): `app/test/FirstTapSequenceTest.kt` (`turningBluetoothOnFromTheCardGoesOnToTheScan`, `theCardFollowsTheAdapterLive`), `app/androidTest/RingStatesRenderTest.kt`. On an API 36.1 emulator the request showed "OpenCircuit wants to turn on Bluetooth" and Allow led on to the scan; Bluetooth turned on from the shell flipped the card from "Bluetooth off" to "Ready". |
| D-249 | **DEVIATION (addition): a scan Android refuses or ends with an error shows "Couldn't search for rings" with the reason and Search again.** Upstream has no scan-failure state (CoreBluetooth reports no scan error; `A/ContentView.swift:1236-1246` falls back to the connect control). `:ble`'s scanner reports `Failed(-1)` when Android refuses to start a scan and `Failed(code)` for a scan error; the card says which ("Android didn't start the scan…" or "…scan error (code N)…") and offers Search again. A scanner that throws is shown as a failed scan and logged; one that ends without an answer reads as no ring found. | A scan that fails must say so rather than look idle. | E8 (done): `app/test/ScanStateTest.kt` (`aScanAndroidRefusedToStartSaysSo`, `aScanErrorShowsItsCode`, `aScannerThatThrowsIsShownAsAFailedScanAndLogged`, `aScanThatEndsWithoutAnAnswerReadsAsNoRingFound`), `app/androidTest/RingStatesRenderTest.kt`. |
| D-250 | **DEVIATION (addition): a chosen ring is paired through Android's companion-device sheet before the first bond, explained first, and the sheet's result code decides.** CoreBluetooth bonds on its own and has no equivalent (upstream connects the chosen ring at once). Here a selected or picked ring first shows "Pair with <ring>", which says what Android's sheet is for (its own summary is Android's generic companion-device text); Continue removes the app's companion-device associations for the ring's address unless the phone is already bonded to it, then asks `CompanionDeviceManager.associate()` for exactly that ring (an address scan filter and the quoted advertised name, single device, no device profile), and the activity shows the sheet with `StartIntentSenderForResult`. The sheet's result code decides: `RESULT_OK` remembers the ring and connects; `RESULT_USER_REJECTED` (1) or `RESULT_CANCELED` (0) shows "Pairing cancelled" with nothing saved or connected; a discovery timeout (2), an internal (3) or security (4) error, an unknown code, a failure before the sheet, a refused request or a phone without `android.software.companion_device_setup` remember and connect anyway, with the reason shown in Connection details. A failure the callback reports after the sheet opened is not the answer: the result code that follows is. The association id is never saved; associations are looked up by address. | An association approved less than ten minutes before the app asks to bond lets Android bond without a consent prompt, and a stale association for the same address found first defeats that. Where the sheet cannot help, Android's own pairing prompt still lets the user pair. | E8 (done): `app/test/CompanionPairingFlowTest.kt` (17), `app/test/ScanStateTest.kt`, `app/androidTest/RingStatesRenderTest.kt`; red when a refusal (result 1) connects and when hygiene ignores the bond. On an API 36 emulator the sheet showed "Looking for a device"; its Cancel returned result 1, and the card read "Pairing cancelled". The emulator's sheet did not time out within 143 s with no matching device; on a phone the ring is nearby (the on-phone checks decide the rest). |
| D-251 | **DEVIATION: Stop reconnecting forgets the ring, removes its companion-device associations and retires its link; Cancel and Disconnect keep it remembered.** Upstream's `forgetActiveRing` (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:615-630`), reached from Disconnect, the connect card's Cancel and Stop reconnecting alike, clears the active ring but keeps it in the saved list for a one-tap reconnect. Here Cancel and Disconnect only disconnect (the ring reconnects on the next launch, D-244), and Stop reconnecting stops a running measure, disconnects the link, closes it (its receivers unregistered, never to reconnect), clears `ring.remembered.v1` and removes every companion-device association for the address. The phone's bond is left alone; only Android's Bluetooth settings remove it. The card goes back to "Ready", never to an error. | One ring is remembered at a time (D-246), so letting go of it means forgetting it; a stale association would defeat the next pairing's no-prompt window. | E8 (done): `app/test/RememberedRingStoreTest.kt` (five stop-reconnecting tests), `app/test/LaunchReconnectTest.kt`; red when the disassociate is skipped. |
| D-252 | **DEVIATION: one link per ring, built once and kept; a different ring retires the old link first.** Upstream reconnects its remembered `CBPeripheral` through the system (`A/BLE/RingScanner.swift:96-164`). Here `:ble`'s Android link registers its Bluetooth and bond receivers when it is built and keeps them until it is closed, so the app builds one link per ring and reuses it: opening the app with the remembered ring, reopening the screen, Try again after a failed pairing and pairing the same ring again all connect the same link. Pairing a different ring disconnects and closes the old link before building the new one; forgetting and pairing again builds exactly one new link. Each link's session runs in its own scope, cancelled when the link is retired. | A second link for the same ring would leak its receivers for the life of the process. | E8 (done): `app/test/LaunchReconnectTest.kt` (a counting link factory: one build across launch, reopen and Try again; one more after forget and pair, with the old link closed first), `app/androidTest/FirstRunEndToEndTest.kt`; red when the launch path scans. |
| D-253 | **DEVIATION (addition): a Connection details card in every build, with a Copy that masks the address and the ring's name.** Upstream has no such screen (its developers read the iPhone's logs). The phone here is never on a cable, so the Ring screen shows the link's facts (firmware, ATT MTU, history-safe, bonded, MAC mismatch), the app's own timings (scan to selection, connect to authenticated, the last reconnect wait, the pairing prompt seen and for how long), the pairing sheet's outcome, unhandled frames and handler failures, torn-down connections and their undelivered frames, the link's diagnostics shown as the link wrote them (never parsed, all 64), and the scanner's last advertisement decoded by `AdStructureParser` (flags, service UUIDs, service data, manufacturer data, tx power, name; a malformed element never throws). A surface the link or scanner does not offer reads "not available". The address, and the advertised name whose suffix is the end of the address, are masked when collapsed and in the Copy text, where advertised payloads appear as byte counts and the address and name suffix are scrubbed wherever they occur; full values only on screen when expanded. Copy writes to the clipboard only on the user's tap. | Evidence for the on-phone checks without logcat, without putting the ring's address into text that can be shared. | E8 (done): `app/test/ConnectionDetailsTest.kt` (12), `app/test/AdStructureParserTest.kt` (15), `app/androidTest/ConnectionDetailsRenderTest.kt` (4); red when the Copy text is neither masked nor scrubbed, and when the parser's length bound is removed. |
| D-254 | **DEVIATION (addition): after a cancelled pairing sheet the user can pair without it, recorded as their choice.** The "Pairing cancelled" card (D-250) keeps Scan & connect and gains a second action, "Pair without the system sheet". It remembers and connects the ring the sheet was shown for, as a sheet that could not help does, so the ring bonds through Android's own pairing prompt inside `:ble`; Connection details reads "connected without it: you chose to pair without the system sheet (result N)", N being the sheet's own code. The sheet is not asked for again. The action does nothing unless the pairing was cancelled, and a second tap connects nothing more. | On an API 36 emulator with no matching device the sheet sat on "Looking for a device" for 143 s and never timed out, and its Cancel returns result 1, a refusal. If the sheet's address filter cannot match the real ring, the user would otherwise have no way to pair. A refusal still connects nothing by itself. | E8 (done): `app/test/CompanionPairingFlowTest.kt` (21), `app/test/ConnectionDetailsTest.kt`, `app/androidTest/RingStatesRenderTest.kt`; red when the cancelled card has no second action and when the action neither remembers nor connects the ring. |
